According to whether clear realization is present, what is coarse in a person’s continuum is designated subtle when present in one with realization. Cognitive obscurations too are exclusively the three poisonous afflictions. In the Vast Play, a week after awakening, [the Buddha says]:

<!-- pair: TGD-001968 -->
Stupidity arising, desire, and anger—
These are like enemies of beings.
I have destroyed them here without remainder.

<!-- pair: TGD-001971 -->
And the Cloud of Jewels says:

<!-- pair: TGD-001973 -->
When you abided at awakening’s heart,[^N-B-026]
Desire, hatred, stupidity,
And the many other stains to abandon
Were burned by the fire of your primordial knowing.

<!-- pair: TGD-001977 -->
Because this is taught, the three obscurations are one as afflictive obscuration.

<!-- pair: TGD-001979 -->
Vajra statement. Others hold that the truth of the nature of phenomena is realized by each higher system refuting the lower, from outsiders through Atiyoga. Taking that as the subject: it follows that the nature of phenomena is not realized, because this is constructed by the conceptual mind and has become an object of comprehension and a valid cognition.[^N-B-027] All tenet systems are views and conceptual mind. That does not know the ultimate. Engaging in Bodhisattva Activity says:

<!-- pair: TGD-001985 -->
The ultimate is not the conceptual mind’s sphere.

<!-- pair: TGD-001986 -->
And so forth. The Root Verses on Discerning Knowing say, “Having been embraced by his loving awakened intention …,” and so forth. The Condensed Perfection of Discerning Knowing says:

<!-- pair: TGD-001991 -->
However one apprehends, that is taught as thorough affliction;
Not taking self and mine as objects of focus
Is called complete purification.

<!-- pair: TGD-001994 -->
The Commentary on Valid Cognition says, “When there is a self …,” and so forth. Thus every tenet system obscures seeing ultimate truth.

<!-- pair: TGD-001998 -->
Vajra statement. Two positions occur: that hearers do not see selflessness, and that they realize it completely. It is also said that because directional parts and moments refute their partless ultimate, they do not see the truth.

<!-- pair: TGD-002002 -->
Here hearers too see one aspect of the natural state. Without the discerning knowing that realizes the natural state, they could not transcend samsara, just like outsiders. The noble Sutra of the Ten Levels says that they are surpassed by the conceptual mind of the seventh level, Far Gone; this clearly establishes the point. Otherwise they would be surpassed by the conceptual mind of the first level, since [realization] would be absent in hearers and present in bodhisattvas. The master too says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002012 -->
In the Greater Vehicle, non-arising is taught;
For the others, exhaustion is emptiness.
Since exhaustion and non-arising
Are one in meaning, accept this.

<!-- pair: TGD-002016 -->
And: “Blessed One, entities and nonentities … knowing …,” and so forth.

<!-- pair: TGD-002019 -->
Yet their realization is not complete. The paths, results, and qualities of the Greater and Lesser Vehicles are said to differ like the ocean and water in a hoofprint, or space and the space inside a mustard seed eaten by a worm.

<!-- pair: TGD-002024 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that of the Greater and Lesser Vehicles the Lesser is extremely inferior. The Compendium of the Greater Vehicle says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002026 -->
Going to hell is not
An enduring obstacle to buddhahood;
The two levels of hearers
And solitary buddhas are enduring obstacles.

<!-- pair: TGD-002030 -->
On that basis others call it inferior because it does not see the first level’s truth. Here, clear realization through the sixth level is shared with hearers and solitary realizers. The Sutra of the Ten Levels says that the fifth level realizes the four truths, and all scriptures say hearers too realize those. Likewise its statement that the sixth level realizes the twelve links of dependent arising is equal to the statement that solitary buddhas realize them.

<!-- pair: TGD-002037 -->
For example, the sons of a king and a minister may be the same age. Are their qualities equal? They are not. The Precious Garland says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002041 -->
In that vehicle of hearers,

<!-- pair: TGD-002042 -->
“The ordinary mind of awakening …,” and “Without realizing signlessness …,” and so forth.

<!-- pair: TGD-002044 -->
Vajra statement. Taking as the subject the Ordinary-Mind-Only school’s claim that knowing empty of apprehended object and apprehending subject, self-aware and self-clear, is truly established: others say it is not established as self-aware and self-clear because what is aware and what it is aware of conflict. Object and object-possessor are relative, and self-awareness is not established; therefore they do not see the truth of the nature of phenomena.[^N-B-028]

<!-- pair: TGD-002050 -->
Here the Ordinary-Mind-Only school’s clear realization equals the clear realization of the bodhisattva’s seventh level, Far Gone. The Descent to Lanka says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002052 -->
Ordinary-Mind-Only is the seventh;
Absence of appearance is the eighth level.
Two levels are abodes;
The eleventh is the level of great primordial knowing.
That is my level.

<!-- pair: TGD-002057 -->
So it is taught. At the sixth level, discerning knowing becomes manifest: realization of dependent arising in forward and reverse order is called discerning knowing that realizes ignorance. Realizing external and internal phenomena as ordinary mind, one can transform anything; that is the perfection of means.

<!-- pair: TGD-002061 -->
The Extensive Sutra too says: “Children of the victors, the three realms are ordinary mind alone. The three times too are realized as equal to ordinary mind. That ordinary mind too is comprehended as without edge or center.” This is also explained well in the passage, “The teaching of Ordinary-Mind-Only teaches the Middle Way.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002068 -->
Vajra statement. Samsara’s root is the personal self, held to be permanent, unitary, and autonomous: strongly clinging to the appropriated aggregates, one apprehends a self as a substance, a succession, an imputation, and so forth. Even without an apprehended personal self, claiming that external and internal phenomena arising merely through dependent arising exist is the self of phenomena. Protector Maitreya and Nagarjuna are said to contradict one another because they maintain, respectively, that its lack of intrinsic nature is realized at the first level and at the eighth.

<!-- pair: TGD-002075 -->
Here the two do not conflict. Their enlightened intent agrees that the absence of intrinsic nature in the self of phenomena is made manifest at the eighth level. If both kinds of selflessness were realized at the first level, the designation Far Gone would have no application. The enlightened intent concerning the first level is realization that the personal self apprehended in the aggregates lacks intrinsic nature. Realization that even the mere appearance of dependently arising phenomena lacks intrinsic nature—the selflessness of phenomena—occurs at the eighth. Both have the same enlightened intent. The Vast Play says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002085 -->
When I attained forbearance that here
There is neither birth nor death,
And all these phenomena lack intrinsic nature and self,
Buddha Dipankara gave me his prediction.

<!-- pair: TGD-002089 -->
And the Sutra of the Questions of Vikurvanaraja says: “Only after realizing the selflessness of phenomena did I see a buddha.” Asked, “Did you not see one during two incalculable eons?” he answered, “I saw the transitory collection.” The Prediction of Dipankara says the same.

<!-- pair: TGD-002095 -->
Statements that both are realized at the “first” refer to the eighth level, which is first among the pure levels and the youthful levels. Such scriptural passages and reasoning establish this.

<!-- pair: TGD-002098 -->
Vajra statement. Others maintain that, among the ten levels and five paths, accumulation and preparation are worldly: the name “path” is applied to their cause. Seeing and cultivation are actual paths beyond the world; the final path is the result designated as a path. The ten levels are stages of clear realization. Beginning with the path of seeing, one proceeds through distinct [deep absorptions], such as Heroic Progress and finally the vajralike deep absorption; when the higher is realized, the lower is discarded.

<!-- pair: TGD-002105 -->
Here one traverses every level and path up to their culmination through the single essence of clear realization of the truth on the path of seeing. The King of Deep Absorption says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002107 -->
Whoever holds that supreme deep absorption
Will attain Radiant, Hard to Purify, Manifest,
Far Gone, Unmoving, Good Conceptual Mind,
And Cloud of Dharma—the ten levels.

<!-- pair: TGD-002111 -->
Applied to the four yogas: when co-emergent primordial knowing without elaboration becomes a little clear, there is one-pointedness; seeing its essence is freedom from conceptual elaborations; realizing all appearance as that itself is one taste; realizing equipoise and post-cultivation attainment, or object and object-possessor, as nondual is non-cultivation.[^N-B-029] It is like designating ten stages, differing until one has crossed the eighteen emptinesses and calm abiding.[^N-B-030]

<!-- pair: TGD-002118 -->
Addendum. Some say that although deep absorptions are numerous, vajralike deep absorption is the one at the end of the succession. Here all deep absorptions are vajralike: the path of seeing arises in dependence on the calm-abiding deep absorption of accumulation and preparation, and through it one traverses up to the tenth level.

<!-- pair: TGD-002124 -->
Vajra statement. Others say: “Immediately upon attaining the first level, one sees a hundred buddhas’ faces and twelve sets of a hundred qualities arise. If you call your one taste pure clear realization, it does not possess even the first level’s qualities.” Here, after attaining a level it must still be purified. There are six alternatives concerning how qualities arise: gradually, all at once, some present and some absent, present but unseen by others, the yogin’s experiential appearances, and scriptural testimony.[^N-B-031]

<!-- pair: TGD-002132 -->
First, qualities arise as described for someone like Shakyamuni, who traversed the path gradually over three incalculable eons.

<!-- pair: TGD-002134 -->
Second, as with Avalokiteshvara, who is said to have attained the eighth level through a single arousal of the ordinary mind [of awakening]; and the old woman, who attained forbearance concerning non-arising phenomena but had not even the first level’s qualities.[^N-B-032]

<!-- pair: TGD-002137 -->
Third, some qualities are present and some absent. Although Sudhana’s gurus were on the tenth level, they solemnly declared, “I know nothing beyond this,” and gave their directions.[^N-B-033]

<!-- pair: TGD-002140 -->
Fourth, qualities exist but are unseen. Nagarjuna is predicted in the Descent to Lanka as being on the first level, in the Great Drum and Manjushri Root Continuum as being on the eighth, and in the Great Cloud as being on the tenth. Yet no one saw the twelve sets of a hundred qualities and so forth. Similarly, Amitabha abided upon Songtsen’s head and Protector Maitreya on Asanga’s shoulder, but were unseen.

<!-- pair: TGD-002147 -->
Fifth, as explained in the Ten Levels, what arises in the ordinary mind of awakening is seen only by the person himself, as with Mila.[^N-B-034]

<!-- pair: TGD-002149 -->
Sixth, sutras and continuums contain testimony that immediately upon attaining a level its qualities arise like the sun and its rays. Therefore one should not cling exclusively to one alternative.

<!-- pair: TGD-002151 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that the accumulation of primordial knowing, the result, arises from the accumulation of merit, the cause, because Engaging in Bodhisattva Activity states:

<!-- pair: TGD-002153 -->
All these branches
The Sage taught for the sake of discerning knowing.

<!-- pair: TGD-002155 -->
And:

<!-- pair: TGD-002156 -->
As long as that root of virtue is not complete,
That holy awakening will not be exhausted.[^N-B-035]

<!-- pair: TGD-002158 -->
So they argue. Here the two accumulations are united and abide inseparably in all three: The Ground, path, and result. The Play of Manjushri says that even at the time of The Ground, “Ignorance itself is awakening; awakening itself is ignorance.” And the Highest Continuum says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002165 -->
Because perfect buddha embodiment proliferates,
Because suchness is inseparable,
And because the lineage is present, all embodied beings
Always possess buddha essence.

<!-- pair: TGD-002169 -->
So it says. On the path too, the Condensed Perfection of Discerning Knowing says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002172 -->
Discerning knowing goes before giving gifts.

<!-- pair: TGD-002173 -->
Thus there is primordial knowing in equipoise and the accumulation of merit in post-cultivation attainment. If one holds that they [proceed] one upon the other, it is like setting down the right and left feet while walking on the ground.[^N-B-036]

<!-- pair: TGD-002177 -->
At the result, the two accumulations reach completion and the two embodiments become manifest. The noble master says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002180 -->
Through this virtue, may all beings …

<!-- pair: TGD-002181 -->
“Merit …,” and so forth. Otherwise cause and result would belong to different kinds, and it would follow that the ultimate is produced by the relative.

<!-- pair: TGD-002184 -->
Vajra statement. Others say: “Of the field, intention, and substance of gathering accumulations, those with a less developed continuum should offer to the perfectly pure field, the Three Supreme Ones, since the result is immeasurable. They rely on the field of spiritual friends to make that a cause of renunciation and great liberation. Bodhisattvas with a more developed continuum cherish inferior fields and choose downward.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002191 -->
Here our position is that, whether the continuum’s development is high or low, one must choose the higher field. A perfectly pure field brings vast temporary and ultimate results; dedication of those roots of virtue to self and others accomplishes great purposes. Giving with the three inferior fields as objects of focus—other [recipients], sentient beings, and those who cause harm—respectively increases afflictions, harms the teaching, and perpetuates harm to oneself.[^N-B-037]

<!-- pair: TGD-002202 -->
Then why is it said that a bodhisattva who judges recipients as good or bad does not complete the perfections? Such inferior recipients must be made perfectly pure in the ordinary mind. All phenomena are formations of ordinary mind, like the dependent arising of an image of an embodiment and its object of focus.

<!-- pair: TGD-002207 -->
Therefore, if one gives to an inferior recipient after arousing the perception of that recipient as a buddha, it will certainly be accomplished accordingly. For people of every degree of development, choosing the higher field and making the inferior field pure is an exceedingly profound essential point.

<!-- pair: TGD-002210 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that to transcend samsara one must abandon self-apprehension. Here, apprehending as self and thereby gathering accumulations in order to abandon that self-apprehension is maintained to be skillful means.[^N-B-038] By apprehending [things] as one’s own and offering them to the Three Supreme Ones, vast results increase. By giving away what was apprehended as one’s own, one realizes selflessness. By apprehending sentient beings as oneself and giving for their sake, one does not abide in peace. Engaging in Bodhisattva Activity likewise says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002222 -->
Taking with my conceptual mind all these unowned things,
I offer them well to the Sages, supreme among beings,
Together with their children.
Holy recipients endowed with great compassionate responsiveness,
In loving enlightened intent toward me, accept these things of mine.

<!-- pair: TGD-002227 -->
So it says. All phenomena of samsara and nirvana are subject to transformation by ordinary mind. Therefore appropriating the happiness, goodness, splendor, and prosperity of samsara and nirvana and gathering accumulations becomes a cause for relinquishing self-apprehension—just as fire arising from wood burns the wood itself. Saraha too says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002233 -->
Ordinary mind itself alone is the seed of everything.

<!-- pair: TGD-002234 -->
“Where …,” and so forth, he says.

<!-- pair: TGD-002235 -->
Vajra statement. Some say one dedicates only virtue one has personally done and gathered, not others’ virtue. Others say that the element of already present virtue cannot be dedicated: whatever merely exists is impermanent; since no phenomenon lies outside the basic space of phenomena, everything would absurdly be virtuous; and since dedication means transformation, if it changed it would become conditioned. Thus, they say, there is nothing to dedicate.

<!-- pair: TGD-002244 -->
Here, if one gathers the roots of virtue of samsara and nirvana into the single mandala of one’s own ordinary mind and dedicates them, that is what comes about. Suchness is said to be undivided, and arousing the ordinary mind [of awakening] is said to make one master of samsara and nirvana. The roots of virtue from that arousal until the holy Dharma disappears are for sentient beings’ sake; therefore past virtue should be dedicated. The Continuum of the Unmoving One says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002252 -->
Through the immeasurable, incalculable merit
Gathered by the Blessed One, /[^N-B-039]
May all beings without exception
Attain the unsurpassed state.

<!-- pair: TGD-002256 -->
And the Extensive Mother also teaches dedicating the roots of virtue of the four noble ones.

<!-- pair: TGD-002258 -->
As for the second position: if mere existence and being accepted entail impermanence, the buddhas’ primordial knowing and ultimate qualities such as their powers would also be impermanent, since they exist and are accepted. If no phenomenon lies outside the basic space of phenomena, why would everything not become stagnant neutrality? The reasoning is the same. Virtue and wrongdoing would then be altogether impossible.[^N-B-040]

<!-- pair: TGD-002265 -->
If dedicating the basic space of phenomena makes everything virtuous and therefore makes it a conditioned object bearing signs, the same applies to virtuous karma; dedication free of poison would become impossible. Dedication must therefore be posited in dependence on the relative: just as merit is made the thing dedicated, it does not proceed according to [ultimately established] characteristics.[^N-B-041] Ultimately, dedication itself is not established, so analysis of whether the basic space of phenomena is something to dedicate does not apply.

<!-- pair: TGD-002273 -->
Therefore the basic space of phenomena, suchness, is to be dedicated: one focuses on all virtue as one taste in the basic space of phenomena and aspires to the natural state, the Dharma embodiment. The Dense Array of Adornments says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002278 -->
The sugata essence, the virtuous element,
Endowed with uncontaminated qualities—
Through dedicating it, the result matures.

<!-- pair: TGD-002281 -->
And: “That sugata essence is also virtue.” In Vajra Banner’s Dedication: “Gathering all the elements together …,” and:

<!-- pair: TGD-002287 -->
Dedicate the virtue arising from yoga;
Dedicate your own ordinary mind as well.

<!-- pair: TGD-002289 -->
And: “Whatever suchness’s intrinsic nature is like, dedicate all actions in that same way.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002293 -->
The Compendium asks, “What is ultimate virtue?” and answers, “Suchness.” There are limitless scriptural passages, such as “the roots of virtue of our unsurpassed primordial knowing.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002298 -->
Although the Mother says that all phenomena, from form to omniscience, are unchanging, there is no contradiction: [dedication] relates to purifying the stains of relative delusion. Thus already present virtue is like the ocean, and virtue done and gathered is like its waves. Therefore dedicate both the gathered and the already present virtue of all samsara and nirvana.

<!-- pair: TGD-002304 -->
Vajra statement. Others say dedication to buddhas and gurus is necessary because faults have been abandoned and qualities perfected.[^N-B-042] Here too dedication for them is certainly necessary, because it speeds the completion of awakened activity, like adding wealth to a wealthy person. The Eight Thousand says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002310 -->
Buddhas’ activity does not depend on a single cause or condition; it is like the sound of a vina.[^N-B-043] Nanda and Nandabala offered the concentrated milk of a thousand cows and dedicated it, saying, “May the observance be accomplished”; through this [the Buddha] awakened at dawn. Likewise, when Devadatta’s hurled stone made his foot bleed, Kashyapa’s declaration of truth stopped it.

<!-- pair: TGD-002318 -->
Gurus too, whether ordinary persons or abiding on the levels, can encounter obstacles; therefore dedication is necessary.

<!-- pair: TGD-002319 -->
This is the fourth section: the explanation of twenty-five vajra statements and one supplementary shloka, gathering the essential points of the ordinary mind of awakening.

<!-- pair: TGD-002320 -->
Although others say that secret mantra teachings occur only with this Teacher, time, and realm, here mantra also occurs with other teachers and teachings. To become a buddha oneself one must abandon every coarse and subtle thing to abandon, and teach that to other disciples. Without mantra there would be no antidote for abandoning the subtle. The Manifest Awakening of Vairocana says:[^N-B-044]

<!-- pair: TGD-002326 -->
Perfect buddhas who have passed,
Those likewise yet to come,
And protectors now present
Who abide for beings’ benefit—
Knowing this excellent rite,
They attain immeasurable omniscience.

<!-- pair: TGD-002332 -->
And “the buddhas of the past …,” and so forth. Therefore mantra occurs elsewhere as well.

<!-- pair: TGD-002335 -->
Addendum. Some say mantra is the fourth scriptural collection, since Excellent Accomplishment says, “Know this from the fourth, the collection of awareness-holders.” Others say it is discipline, because “it disciplines my ordinary mind.” Others say it is sutra, since Subahu says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002340 -->
I have correctly explained secret mantra
In the manner of sutras; listen with a clear disposition.

<!-- pair: TGD-002342 -->
So it says. Again, the Continuum of Clear Realization of Dharma Activity teaches outer and inner abhidharma, so some say mantra is inner abhidharma. Although each explains a particular position, most appear to include it chiefly in sutra.

<!-- pair: TGD-002347 -->
Here mantra is the three scriptural collections, or like the essence of all three. Summarizing what an individual must abandon to awaken, the three trainings—the meaning of the three collections—abandon the three poisons. The union of generation and completion uproots what is to be abandoned, and one awakens in the embodiment of great bliss, the union in which means and discerning knowing are nondual. There is scriptural testimony for all three classifications; on this account they do not conflict, and it is most reasonable.

<!-- pair: TGD-002354 -->
Vajra statement. Mantra has three: The Ground, the three doors primordially abiding as the three vajras; the path, ripening and liberation; and the result, the four embodiments. The Manjushri Root Continuum says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002358 -->
Whoever has not seen the mandala—
The yogin x
With a fist against space x
The water of a mirage x[^N-B-045]

<!-- pair: TGD-002362 -->
Thus empowerment must come first. Others say it is attained when the one conferring it is a vajra master of superior, middling, or inferior capacity; the recipient is a person whose continuum has been purified through going for refuge and so forth; and the rite follows the continuums, properly performing the preparatory arrangements and the main rites of the four empowerments: vase, secret, discerning-knowing [primordial-knowing], and word.

<!-- pair: TGD-002367 -->
Here, through the dependent arising of a qualified master teaching instructions to a disciple endowed with confident devotion, the stains of apprehending the three doors as separate are purified. By pointing them out as the three vajras, certainty and realization arise: this is called attaining the empowerment of meaning.

<!-- pair: TGD-002373 -->
The vase empowerment is the simultaneous arising of certainty that the aggregates, elements, and sense fields are the mandala’s wheel. The secret empowerment is the arising of deep absorption in clarity-emptiness. The discerning-knowing–primordial-knowing empowerment concerns bliss-emptiness. The word empowerment is realization of ordinary mind itself as non-arising. When certainty concerning these meanings of empowerment arises in one’s continuum, empowerment is held to have been attained.

<!-- pair: TGD-002379 -->
Addendum. The three types of person, superior, middling, and inferior, respectively attain empowerment without its being conferred, attain it when it is conferred, and fail to attain it even when it is conferred. Others place the superior faculty first: through confident devotion to the guru, such a person purifies the three doors’ stains and attains empowerment without its being conferred.

<!-- pair: TGD-002385 -->
Here, although one of superior faculty may have that realization, the rite is certainly necessary because the continuum’s rite is profound. Even with superior faculty the person is unripened, so both master and disciple incur the root downfall of divulging secrets. Like the blind leading the blind, such a person has no authorization to confer empowerment for others. Moreover, the rite is buddhas’ primordial knowing; performing it plants a seed of potency in that person’s continuum.

<!-- pair: TGD-002394 -->
Empowerment must also be conferred on those of inferior faculty, because a cause in the form of habitual tendencies cannot fail eventually to give its result. There is no way around relying on a vase empowerment, which is like The Ground or a body.

<!-- pair: TGD-002397 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that, of the four empowerments, the vase empowerment requires an extensive colored-powder mandala with three seats: the seat of buddhas and bodhisattvas, that of awareness women and goddesses, and that of wrathful males and females. If all three are not complete, it cannot be attained through a practice such as Vajrayogini.[^N-B-046]

<!-- pair: TGD-002402 -->
Here buddhas have abandoned the most subtle habitual tendencies and awakened as a single mandala wheel. “Seat” refers to the illusory body as support, with the self-aware ordinary mind of awakening abiding as what is supported.

<!-- pair: TGD-002406 -->
One’s five aggregates as the five buddhas, and the eight collections [of consciousness] as the eight bodhisattvas, are the seat of buddhas and bodhisattvas. The five elements as the five mothers and eight objects as the eight female bodhisattvas are the seat of awareness women and goddesses. The ten great “words” as the ten wrathful males, and ten objects as the ten wrathful females, are the seat of wrathful males and females.[^N-B-047]

<!-- pair: TGD-002412 -->
Whether the Yogini has one deity or five, recognizing the aggregates, elements, and sense fields as buddhas means attaining the vase empowerment; in dependence on that, the other three empowerments are attained. The hundred families, five families, three families, and so forth are included in one family.

<!-- pair: TGD-002419 -->
If one objects, “Though complete implicitly, they are not explicitly complete, so empowerment is not attained,” then except through Manjuvajra it could not be attained through Kalachakra, Guhyasamaja, Chakrasamvara, or Hevajra either, because those are not explicitly complete.[^N-B-048] Therefore, whether the deity number completes one grouping or comprises a single family, empowerment can be conferred and attained.

<!-- pair: TGD-002427 -->
Addendum. Although others maintain that the three embodiments are determined as three kinds of deeds and are inconceivable, here a single deity too accomplishes all awakened activities. Their essence is one and their activities are included without interruption. The Two Examinations says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002431 -->
From the single great ordinary mind
The five aspects of form are displayed;
From those very five families
Many thousands arise.
Therefore all these have one intrinsic nature.

<!-- pair: TGD-002436 -->
So it says. Even the single family of awakened mind contains all five: embodiment, speech, awakened mind, qualities, and activity.[^N-B-049]

<!-- pair: TGD-002439 -->
The defining character of every mantra
Abides in the sugatas’ awakened mind.

<!-- pair: TGD-002441 -->
So it says. Since one deity encompasses all families, it can accomplish all four awakened activities.

<!-- pair: TGD-002444 -->
Addendum. Although others hold that each deity has one definite name, here all deities’ names are assigned in terms of the result made manifest or awakened activity. Therefore even one’s single special deity, such as Chakrasamvara, bears all those names. They are one in purifying obscurations through the path and awakening as the essence of the result’s qualities; in possessing an embodiment with marks and signs and awakened mind with the primordial knowing that is the result of separation [from obscurations]; in being called “Blessed One”; and in caring for those who supplicate them.

<!-- pair: TGD-002453 -->
Vajra statement. The third empowerment, discerning-knowing–primordial-knowing, is said to be received through the seal of another’s body. In the main rite the master’s instructions and subsequent teaching point out the deep absorption of the four joys, bliss-emptiness. Maitripa regards the certainty arising in this way as example primordial knowing, Naropa as meaning primordial knowing; Marpa says this depends on whether that very [reality] is realized. Some accept it as example, others as meaning primordial knowing.[^N-B-050]

<!-- pair: TGD-002459 -->
Here, unless primordial knowing that realizes the meaning of empowerment beyond the conceptual mind has arisen in one’s continuum, the example does not make that meaning comprehensible. All experiential acquaintance that indicates a similarity through something similar is constructed by the conceptual mind. Accomplishment of Primordial Knowing says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002463 -->
The bliss of joining the two organs
Ordinary people call that very [reality];
But the victors have not said
That it is great bliss.

<!-- pair: TGD-002467 -->
So it says. Phagmo Drupa says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002469 -->
Contaminated bliss, at the great gateway …
A limb of great bliss …[^N-B-051]

<!-- pair: TGD-002471 -->
And so forth. Therefore one cannot categorically determine it as either example or meaning. If feeling is realized as co-emergent primordial knowing beyond the conceptual mind, it is example or meaning; if not realized, it is neither.

<!-- pair: TGD-002475 -->
Addendum. Concerning the third empowerment there are four claims: primordial knowing in one’s continuum is not realized through bliss of a similar kind; since it is realized without error it is no different from buddhas’ primordial knowing; it is meaning primordial knowing when bliss-emptiness of equal taste is experienced inexpressibly; or only one ray of the path of seeing’s primordial knowing arises. Those hoping for a better understanding say it realizes a mere reflection. Yangdak Lha says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002484 -->
Whatever arises in the third moment
Is not seeing meaning; with the result-bearing [factor]
It is sullied, like molasses.[^N-B-052]

<!-- pair: TGD-002487 -->
Thus each accepts one or the other, example or meaning. Because it is contaminated bliss, they say one must engage without being stained by the fifth root downfall. Here, at those four times or occasions, there is no occasion permitting [breach of] root samaya. The reason is as the Great Brahmin says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002492 -->
Until yesterday I was not a monk;
From today onward I am a monk.

<!-- pair: TGD-002494 -->
Accordingly, if realization of co-emergent primordial knowing, the equal taste of happiness and suffering, arises in one’s continuum, there is no need to say that it is example and meaning primordial knowing. But under the influence of degeneration, most people invoke the path and earlier masters’ deeds as a pretext while engaging solely in the afflicted tactile pleasure of contaminated sexual union. Those hoping for something better may have bliss arise as co-emergent; yet unless all feeling arises as co-emergent, suffering [must also] arise as co-emergent. If it does not, all the gathered “realizations” are not realized as the nature of phenomena; the third empowerment’s purpose is empty, and root downfalls of sutra and mantra are accomplished.[^N-B-053] Hevajra likewise says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002507 -->
Without semen there is no bliss;
Without bliss, that is absent.

<!-- pair: TGD-002509 -->
And Lord Jowo says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002511 -->
Because the Great Primordial Buddha [Continuum]
Emphatically prohibits them,
The secret and discerning-knowing empowerments
Must not be received by those practising celibacy.

<!-- pair: TGD-002515 -->
Further, in our own system the three vows have one essential point, and what is prohibited is prohibited from the outset. Therefore they should not be received from the beginning.

<!-- pair: TGD-002519 -->
Vajra statement. Of generation and completion, the liberating path for the ripened, the first is discussed in terms of three characteristics. The nature of phenomena is the thoroughly established; dependence on assembled conditions is the other-powered; and imputing what is not so as so is the imputed. In cultivation of a meaning generalization’s aspect, of the two, succession and imputation, it is the former.[^N-B-054] The cultivating knowing is other-powered; since [the deity] is absent in actuality, contriving what is not so as so is imputation. Others compare it to Nagabodhi cultivating a tree and its appearing.

<!-- pair: TGD-002527 -->
Here generation-stage deities are thoroughly established from the beginning, because diligent practice accomplishes them—just as smelting gold ore produces gold. Otherwise they could not be accomplished, just as smelting ordinary stone does not produce gold.

<!-- pair: TGD-002533 -->
If something could be accomplished by imputing what is not so as so, outsiders too would realize the natural state, since they presume what is not the natural state to be it. Therefore all aggregates, elements, and sense fields have primordially abided as the mandala wheel. Hevajra says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002538 -->
Through the power of these, sentient beings
Are undoubtedly buddhas.
The ten months extend the levels;
Sentient beings are lords of the ten levels.[^N-B-055]

<!-- pair: TGD-002542 -->
Guhyasamaja says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002543 -->
In brief, the five aggregates
Are renowned as the five buddhas.

<!-- pair: TGD-002545 -->
And Universal Activity says, “These beings are the identity of the five buddhas.” The Ocean of Secrets says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002549 -->
All these mandalas of the knowable, without exception,
Are the thoroughly established three embodiments: the secret-mantra vehicle.
Entering the three embodiments’ realm is the vehicle of the path.
The three embodiments without meeting or parting are the vajra vehicle.

<!-- pair: TGD-002553 -->
So it says.

<!-- pair: TGD-002554 -->
Addendum. Others say one cannot enter deity [practice] at the outset, but must cultivate through training in purification, alternation, illusion, and so forth. First one purifies through regarding the environment as the immeasurable palace and its inhabitants as male and female deities. In the middle one trains in alternating those two, and finally in mere illusion. Only then, they say, does cultivation of one’s own body as deity become clear.

<!-- pair: TGD-002562 -->
Here, because generation stage is thoroughly established, entering all at once into the inseparability of one’s own body and the deity is the profound essential point. The awareness-holder’s vow is not to let body, speech, and mental faculty slip into ordinary [appearance] for even an instant.

<!-- pair: TGD-002567 -->
Addendum. Concerning accomplishing the deity, Samputa says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002569 -->
Recite a hundred thousand for the lord of the wheel,
And likewise ten thousand for those of the mandala.

<!-- pair: TGD-002571 -->
On that basis, others say approach recitation is foremost. Here, too, one must know with certainty that accomplishment comes from the full assembly of dependent-arising factors, with the thoroughly established generation stage as The Ground.

<!-- pair: TGD-002574 -->
The inner dependent-arising factors are the five manifest awakenings: emptiness as The Ground, the moon as seat, syllables as speech, the emblem as awakened mind, and the fully complete embodiment. The five aggregates’ inherent presence is the cause; the guru’s pointing out through vase empowerment is the condition. Knowing that it is thoroughly established through the power of this assembly constitutes outer and inner dependent arising.

<!-- pair: TGD-002580 -->
The number of approach recitations is only one part of this.

<!-- pair: TGD-002581 -->
Addendum. Others say: “Without hardship one does not awaken; austerity must be like ‘water-drinking stone.’[^N-B-056] Relying on sense pleasures makes the body arrogant and generates afflictions; when the body declines, they do not arise. When afflictions arise, one should abase and censure oneself.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002586 -->
Here such ascetic activity amounts to disparagement, because it imputes an ordinary body. Generation stage is thoroughly established; Buddhists chiefly discipline the ordinary mind within; and [such disparagement] incurs a root downfall. “Completely discipline your own ordinary mind,” it is said. Shantideva says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002594 -->
Apart from the observance of guarding ordinary mind,
What use are many observances?

<!-- pair: TGD-002596 -->
And: “The aggregates are the identity of the five buddhas.” Therefore, when ordinary mind is disciplined, body and speech too become disciplined.

<!-- pair: TGD-002600 -->
Vajra statement. Some say that those with the power of former training, whose previous karmic impetus has awakened, can develop deep absorption without relying on a spiritual friend or instructions, so entry rites are unnecessary. Here even those in whom deep absorption arises naturally in this way need the entry rites exactly as explained, from going for refuge to empowerment. Superimpositions about whether [their experience] is genuine are cut through, and further development expands it still more, like adding wood to a blazing fire.

<!-- pair: TGD-002608 -->
Thus going for refuge impresses the Buddhist seal; individual liberation gives definite emergence from samsara; arousing the ordinary mind [of awakening] prevents deviation into the hearer or solitary-realizer path; and mantra empowerment reverses the three doors’ collapse into an ordinary state. Pointing out the meaning of great bliss, inseparable from the deep absorption of generation and completion, brings attainment of supreme and common accomplishments.

<!-- pair: TGD-002614 -->
Although a human body is attained through previously guarding ethical discipline, without continuing the proliferation of virtue and practising on that support, one falls into lower destinies. It is like that.

<!-- pair: TGD-002617 -->
Vajra statement. Others say mantra deities conform to disciples: paired in union for those with desire, wrathful for those with hatred, and bearing animal heads for those with stupidity, and so appear even without major and minor marks.

<!-- pair: TGD-002621 -->
Here all deities possess the qualities of major and minor marks through their intrinsic nature, and they must be cultivated that way.[^N-B-057] They are buddhas who have abandoned faults and completed qualities. Cultivating them with excellent marks completes the accumulations; without those marks, faults arise. Kalachakra says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002627 -->
The supreme lord of victors’
Thirty-two major marks
And eighty excellent minor signs—
All gathered without exception—
Constitute the supreme sovereign’s state,
Which mantra practitioners should cultivate.

<!-- pair: TGD-002633 -->
The Vajra Pavilion says, “That embodiment adorned with thirty-two excellent marks and eighty minor signs is me.” Hevajra says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002637 -->
In the queen’s bhaga, the abode of bliss,
The Teacher with thirty-two marks,
The chief with eighty minor signs,
Abides in the aspect called “Guwa.”[^N-B-058]

<!-- pair: TGD-002641 -->
Such passages are limitless. Without understanding this manner, cultivating paired deities in union, terrifying deities, or animal aspects while motivated by the three poisons is no different from propitiating gyal and sen spirits. Even if stability is attained, not a single quality arises.

<!-- pair: TGD-002647 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that whether cultivating a deity oneself, showing it to others, or painting its embodiment, one should reproduce whatever face one has seen through strenuous practice. Here one must chiefly follow only those deities’ forms, proportions, and other features exactly as taught in stainless sutras and continuums, since the knower of all knowable things teaches all sentient beings the means of abandoning faults and accomplishing qualities.

<!-- pair: TGD-002654 -->
What is wrong with the deity one has oneself seen? One’s continuum is obscured; the fourfold combination of afflictions produces various visual appearances. Unless what one sees is brought into accordance with the victors’ word, one does not know whether it leads to good or harm; it is mistaken knowing that conflicts with their word.[^N-B-059]

<!-- pair: TGD-002660 -->
Vajra statement. Others assign respectively to superior, middling, and inferior faculties: mandalas of heaps, painted cloth, and colored powder; generation stage completed through instantaneous recollection, through a seed syllable, and through cultivating the elements in successive layers; and no rite, a middling rite, and an extensive rite. For superior faculties, they say, the first in each of the three groups—heaps and so forth—is sufficient.

<!-- pair: TGD-002666 -->
Here an extensive rite is especially necessary for those of superior faculty. Realization of non-arising depends on the dependent arising of cause and result; when it is realized, it appears as cause and result, and all qualities and awakened activities arise. Those of inferior faculty do not know how to engage in this way. The Mother says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002672 -->
“Because all phenomena are evenness, the perfection of discerning knowing is evenness.” Also: “Because space is boundless x”; “Mount Meru’s diversity x.”[^N-B-060]

<!-- pair: TGD-002676 -->
These statements teach that point.

<!-- pair: TGD-002677 -->
Addendum. Other scholars say: “Doing, Activity, and Yoga are not profound, while Unsurpassed is profound, because the former have interpretable meaning and the latter definitive meaning.”[^N-B-061] Here we agree that it is definitive meaning; nevertheless both the three of interpretable meaning and Unsurpassed of definitive meaning require extensive rites. Without causes and conditions there is no accomplishment, just as a seed without water, manure, warmth, and moisture does not grow.

<!-- pair: TGD-002684 -->
Therefore the definitive meaning—that one’s own body is primordially the three vajras—must be realized through rites. Once realized, the methods of interpretable meaning, such as rites, become one taste with definitive meaning.

<!-- pair: TGD-002688 -->
Addendum. Others say one must enter through an extensive rite at the very outset of empowerment. Here a concise rite too is accepted if that concise method can discipline clinging to signs. When ordinary mind is held on a single realization and the guru points it out, realization is attained without difficulty, as with Nagabodhi and others.[^N-B-062]

<!-- pair: TGD-002694 -->
This comes from the assembled dependent-arising factors: a qualified guru, the disciple’s confident devotion, and previous training.

<!-- pair: TGD-002697 -->
Addendum. Of the two hindrances to mantra, enemies and obstructors, others consider it profound to cultivate protection against obstructors as a wheel containing a mountain of fire, a vajra fence, ten wrathful deities, and an immeasurable palace. Here those are partial. The extraordinary protection wheel is the nondual ordinary mind of awakening. In ultimate truth, it is said, there are neither obstructors nor even a buddha.

<!-- pair: TGD-002703 -->
Although they do not exist, obstructing appearances arise from the assembly of object, faculty, and knowing. Their root is the mental faculty’s sense field: the eye and other faculties are taken together with mental consciousness, giving the six that arise and spread toward external form. Eye, ear, nose, tongue, body, and mental faculty are taught as six vajras encompassing all sense fields of stupidity, hatred, miserliness, desire, and jealousy, respectively.[^N-B-063]

<!-- pair: TGD-002709 -->
Outwardly, the eye is Kshitigarbha and its family Vairocana. Through habituation and familiarity with this as one’s own Kshitigarbha, all that is seen does not depart from Vairocana; what is called an obstruction has never been established.

<!-- pair: TGD-002712 -->
Likewise apply the remaining five as above: the ear and so on are respectively Lord of Secrets, Akashagarbha, Avalokiteshvara, Dispeller of Obscurations, and Samantabhadra, who gathers the sense fields. Their families are Akshobhya, Ratnasambhava, Amitabha, Amoghasiddhi, and, as the sixth, Vajradhara.

<!-- pair: TGD-002714 -->
Inwardly, the five primordial knowings—mirrorlike, basic space of phenomena, evenness, discriminating primordial knowing, and accomplishing activity—subdue the five ignorances to be abandoned: stupidity, hatred, miserliness, desire, and jealousy. They do so without removing or installing anything, without setting up an object to abandon and an antidote; this suffices as protection. “Protection” means protecting through the path what abides as cause, and thereby making the result manifest.[^N-B-064]

<!-- pair: TGD-002717 -->
Vajra statement. Others say the extensive presentations of the preliminary and main ripening rites, liberating generation and completion stages, and so on are taught for those who enjoy elaboration. Here all those stages of elaboration are naturally occurring dependent-arising factors present in the character of the knowable. The five in the outer environment—wind, fire, water, earth, and Mount Meru—are taught as the soles, genitals, navel, chest, and spinal joints of one’s own body; one must cultivate them accordingly.

<!-- pair: TGD-002724 -->
As for the formation of the inhabitants, cultivating male and female deities’ intrinsic nature as primordially established makes the result, evenness, manifest. These elaborations abide primordially and arise through dependent arising. Likewise:

<!-- pair: TGD-002728 -->
Other than dependent arising,
There is no phenomenon whatsoever.

<!-- pair: TGD-002730 -->
And the Kila Continuum Rays of the Sun says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002732 -->
Through phenomena abiding as dependent arising,
Awareness’s primordial knowing becomes clear.

<!-- pair: TGD-002734 -->
So it says.

<!-- pair: TGD-002735 -->
Vajra statement. Others say the vajra body’s natural state consists of channels that form and abide together with the body, moving winds, and the arrangement of the ordinary mind of awakening’s spheres in four wheels.[^N-B-065] Here the profound vajra body is held to be the nondual wheel, because it is the Dharma embodiment, like a diamond endowed with seven assertions. The Vajra Peak says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002742 -->
Hard, substantial, without a hollow interior,
Not to be broken or destroyed,
Not to be burned, without fear—
Emptiness is explained as vajra.

<!-- pair: TGD-002746 -->
Thus it is the Dharma embodiment together with qualities and deeds. One who never lets the three doors depart from the three vajras is called a vajra-holder. Through this, making manifest the inseparable three doors as the indivisible vajra of nonduality is called the vajra body.

<!-- pair: TGD-002750 -->
The bodyless body, the supreme body;
The one who realizes the body’s limit.

<!-- pair: TGD-002752 -->
As this states, it is the manifestation of that.

<!-- pair: TGD-002753 -->
Vajra statement. Others say the natural state of channels, winds, and spheres is determined solely by what the continuums teach. Here Vajradhara concealed certain profound aspects of the natural state by scrambling the continuums, twisting the scriptural transmissions, and hiding the instructions.[^N-B-066] Sharp-facultied people who enter through intellectual understanding without relying on a guru will fail to attain accomplishment and will deteriorate.

<!-- pair: TGD-002759 -->
Dharma depends on scriptural transmission; transmission on instructions; instructions on lineage gurus. The meaning is concealed so that those of sharp faculties realize its profundity by relying on a guru and practising diligently. The Vajra Essence says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002767 -->
The eighty thousand Dharma collections
And four thousand, counted in thousands,[^N-B-067]
I have taught as perfectly gathered.
This meaning is not proclaimed elsewhere;
So that disciples who respect
Their gurus may realize it,
It is concealed in other continuums.

<!-- pair: TGD-002774 -->
The manner of realization is like that of the precious Dharma Lord.

<!-- pair: TGD-002775 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that because the body’s faults and qualities arise from the dependent arising of channels and winds, the continuum teachings on the causes of its initial formation, The Ground of its intervening abiding, and the conditions of its final destruction are profound.

<!-- pair: TGD-002780 -->
Here the medical continuums are held to be profound concerning certain modes of presence and functioning of channels and winds—the doing, doer, and activity. They explain clearly what the mantra continuums conceal and do not teach clearly. For example, the Eight Branches explains initial formation according to whichever of the three poisons predominates, the three disorders’ “skin,” their alterations, the respective antidotes and means of clearing them that purify faults and generate benefit and happiness, and which wind abides in which channel.[^N-B-068]

<!-- pair: TGD-002788 -->
This too is buddhas’ awakened activity: the meaning taught in scriptures such as Golden Light and explained by the bodhisattvas of the three families, master Nagarjuna, and others.

<!-- pair: TGD-002791 -->
Vajra statement. Others regard going for refuge, the Dharma wheel of the four truths, the discipline collection, the training in ethical discipline, the individual-liberation vow, arousing the ordinary mind [of awakening], the ultimate, Unsurpassed Continuum, the fourth empowerment, the liberating path, and completion stage as profound, and other things as not profound.[^N-B-069]

<!-- pair: TGD-002796 -->
Here those practices others regard as not profound are made profound. Without them one cannot attain even the path’s qualities, let alone buddhahood, like branches and leaves severed from the root. Therefore going for refuge is profound, because it is the foundational Ground of all Dharma—just as a universal monarch must first be born into the proper family in order to appear in the world.

<!-- pair: TGD-002806 -->
Thereafter the four truths, discipline, and so forth are profound, just as that [monarch] must rely on special wheels of Dharma, crafts, letters, and reckoning. Lord Jigten Gonpo says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002809 -->
Though others call higher rites and empowerments profound,
My child, I place great weight on the vase empowerment.[^N-B-070]
It is like The Ground, a vessel, and a body;
The others are its special qualities.

<!-- pair: TGD-002813 -->
So he says. The Two Examinations likewise teaches from “First, confer restoration and purification,” through to “After that, commence Hevajra.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002819 -->
Vajra statement. Others say the practices just described as not profound are unnecessary: practising channels and winds progressively dissolves the four courses, traverses up to the tenth level, makes manifest the result’s four embodiments and five primordial knowings, and attains Vajradhara’s state.

<!-- pair: TGD-002825 -->
Here, without those supposedly unprofound practices, instructions on channels and winds do not bring buddhahood, because the supporting Ground is absent. A universal monarch likewise cannot live without food. A diligent person might attain some higher knowing and miraculous powers, but outsiders have these too; even they require freedom from desire, which appears difficult nowadays.

<!-- pair: TGD-002833 -->
Addendum. Although others regard mantra’s distinctive instructions on channels and winds as profound, here discipline surpasses the essential point of any mantra instruction. Restraining the three doors’ faults makes body, speech, and mental faculty workable. It purifies the faults: unchastity in body and channels, falsehood in speech and winds, covetous ordinary mind and the rest in mental faculty and spheres. The pure essence of qualities increases, filling the four wheels; the four meditative stabilities, four results of spiritual discipline, and ultimately the powers and other qualities arise.

<!-- pair: TGD-002843 -->
If the three doors are not purified, the three poisons make them turbid. A monk’s firm ordinary mind of restraint restrains all three—channels, winds, and spheres. This is also why the four wheels are related to the four schools.[^N-B-071]

<!-- pair: TGD-002846 -->
Addendum. The all-basis, the sugata essence, is realized only partially even at the tenth level, like an elephant in a dark enclosure. “Like the sun in a sky where clouds are seen …”; “Here even you intelligent noble ones, whose conceptual mind’s eye is pure, do not see all.”[^N-B-072] This accords with the general position.

<!-- pair: TGD-002852 -->
Here, however, through diligently cultivating the meaning of instructions taught by a guru endowed with blessing, the all-basis is sometimes seen in another way. When familiarization reaches the essential point, the elements gather inward. One sees in the body the seeds of habitual tendencies as channel knots, and the seeds of the six awarenesses abiding as syllables. When winds enter there, the six families are seen at their respective places. Through long familiarization, one sees the six awarenesses’ mandalas outwardly and much of what arises from particular outer causes and conditions. This is seeing the all-basis, or the nature of phenomena, the sugata essence, because phenomena’s dependent arising is made manifest.[^N-B-073]

<!-- pair: TGD-002864 -->
Purifying the all-basis, afflicted mental faculty, mental consciousness, and five gates gives rise to the five primordial knowings: basic space of phenomena, mirrorlike, evenness, and the rest. Therefore one must distinguish the senses of all-basis that is not seen even at the tenth level, seen at the seventh, seen at the first, and seen by ordinary persons.

<!-- pair: TGD-002870 -->
Vajra statement. Others call profound a great cultivator’s experiential acquaintance even when it occurs in a form explained in neither the Buddha’s word nor treatises. Here experiential acquaintance conflicting with the word is mistaken conceptual thought, since it conflicts with the omniscient one’s speech or has taken up an outsider’s path. It is like outsiders’ tenets imputed through higher knowing, concerning the destruction of the former limit and nondestruction of the latter.[^N-B-074]

<!-- pair: TGD-002877 -->
An experience opposed to the speech of the Buddha, who has great knowing and love, is the work of “bowing” [apparently Mara].[^N-B-075] Understand this according to the Mother and the Nine-Volume Ushnisha.

<!-- pair: TGD-002880 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that because the scriptural collections and continuum classes, and interpretable and definitive meanings, differ, what is prohibited in one is permitted in another, and what is abandoned in one is accomplished in another. Partial agreement is sufficient, and lower teachings are discarded on entering higher ones.

<!-- pair: TGD-002885 -->
Here one must understand all enlightened intent as free of contradiction. The eighty thousand Dharma collections were taught as antidotes to afflictions; all must be practised to abandon faults and accomplish qualities. The three vows and Mahamudra have the same essential point of ethical discipline, and the meaning they express is one. The Moon Lamp says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002891 -->
Whatever sutras I have taught,
Their syllables differ, but their meaning is one.

<!-- pair: TGD-002893 -->
And Jowo says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002895 -->
The eighty thousand Dharma collections
And the four thousand that were taught
All incline and flow into this nature of phenomena.

<!-- pair: TGD-002898 -->
Therefore they must be understood without contradiction.

<!-- pair: TGD-002899 -->
Vajra statement. Others say the faults and qualities of practice concern particular instructions and occur solely according to them. Here they are held to occur according to the omniscient victor’s word, rather than merely according to instructions. Through direct perception of every dependent arising of cause and result, he knows precisely which essential points generate qualities and abandon faults, and teaches them to disciples with great love. Thus they occur exactly as scripture states.

<!-- pair: TGD-002906 -->
Vajra statement. Others accept three deep absorptions as causes of the three embodiments: non-conceptuality, in which not even the slightest conceptual thought moves; bliss, in which even a knife-strike to the body is blissful; and clarity, without distinction of outer, inner, or individual.

<!-- pair: TGD-002910 -->
Here those three faultless deep absorptions are causes or seeds of samsara’s three realms: bliss gives birth in the desire realm, clarity in the form realm, and non-conceptuality in the formless realm. Aryadeva says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002915 -->
Although the forest fire of meditative stability repeatedly burns afflictions’ thicket,
Because the firm root of self-view is not abandoned,
It grows again at once, as if watered by rain.

<!-- pair: TGD-002918 -->
Protector Phagmo Drupa too says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002920 -->
In the ocean of bliss and clarity
Abides the jewel of clinging and the apprehending subject.
If strong clinging to that jewel arises,
Hell’s guardians, like poisonous snakes,
Cut off the life of great bliss
And cast one into a terrifying abyss.

<!-- pair: TGD-002926 -->
Accordingly, I think what is needed is the three as inseparable, of equal taste in the basic space of phenomena. Though elsewhere they are described as faultless, I wonder whether the enlightened intent is that they have not gone beyond conceptual thought apprehending bliss, clarity, and the rest separately.

<!-- pair: TGD-002929 -->
Vajra statement. Using a mandala wheel such as Chakrasamvara as illustration, others say [the principal deity] is the complete enjoyment embodiment, the mother is the Dharma embodiment, and the heroes, dakinis, and gate and intermediate-direction goddesses of the awakened-mind, speech, and embodiment wheels abiding at the twenty-four places are the emanation embodiment; these three are distinct.[^N-B-076]

<!-- pair: TGD-002932 -->
Here each of the three embodiments includes the other two. The mandala, together with its eight charnel grounds, abides in one’s own body. Through cultivating it as the path, the aggregates, elements, and sense fields become perfectly pure, and there is no separation within that wheel. The Ocean of Secrets says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002938 -->
The thoroughly established three embodiments are the secret-mantra vehicle.
Entering those three embodiments is the vehicle of the path.
The three embodiments without meeting or parting are the resultant vehicle.

<!-- pair: TGD-002941 -->
So it teaches.

<!-- pair: TGD-002942 -->
Vajra statement. Others say: “Since the Sage’s and Vajradhara’s teachings, sutra and mantra, are distinct, mantra does not require ethical discipline. Guhyasamaja says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002944 -->
Relying on the five kinds
Of sense pleasure, accomplishment comes swiftly.

<!-- pair: TGD-002946 -->
And, “You should kill living beings,” and so forth.”[^N-B-077]

<!-- pair: TGD-002949 -->
Here ethical discipline is indispensable to secret mantra. To attain Vajradhara’s state in one lifetime, one must abandon faults and accomplish qualities, keeping the three doors unstained by even the slightest fault. Kalachakra says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002954 -->
One endowed with the five training rules,
The bodhisattva vow,
And secret-mantra samaya
Is a lay vajra-holder.

<!-- pair: TGD-002958 -->
The ten trainings x
The bodhisattva’s x
Secret mantra x
A novice x[^N-B-078]

<!-- pair: TGD-002962 -->
Ten million trainings x
Awakening x
Secret mantra x
A fully ordained monk x[^N-B-079]

<!-- pair: TGD-002966 -->
Also, from “First, confer restoration and purification,” through “After that, commence Hevajra.” And the Manjushri Root Continuum says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002972 -->
With corrupted ethical discipline, mantra is not accomplished.

<!-- pair: TGD-002973 -->
So it says.

<!-- pair: TGD-002974 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that in mantra entry, path, and result are blissful, so accomplishment comes through enjoying sense pleasures. Here sutra and mantra are the same regarding desire’s obstructing power. In the vehicle of characteristics, the Mother says: “If desire obstructs even birth in Brahma’s world, what need to mention nirvana?” Also: “Desires are like poisonous leaves, a sword’s edge, and a cavity of filth.” And, as just explained, ethical discipline is indispensable in mantra.

<!-- pair: TGD-002984 -->
The Higher Examination says: “Blessed One, what is impure?” He replied, “The five, form and the rest.” Asked why, he said, “Because their essence is apprehension of form.”

<!-- pair: TGD-002990 -->
The Heap of Jewels says:

<!-- pair: TGD-002991 -->
Whatever obstructing qualities
I have taught to people,
All those I declare truly:
They do not become otherwise.

<!-- pair: TGD-002995 -->
So it says. Instructions to rely on sense pleasures concern a person with a powerful antidote, whose continuum is not stained, like a lotus in mud. Yet this appears far more difficult than the vehicle of characteristics. Therefore this swordlike mantra deteriorates if it does not rely on shieldlike ethical discipline.

<!-- pair: TGD-003001 -->
Vajra statement. Others say that discipline abandons The Ground, the vehicle of characteristics purifies The Ground, and mantra transforms The Ground: desire is transformed into the path as blissful deep absorption, whose result is complete enjoyment embodiment; hatred and stupidity are transformed into the path as deep absorptions of clarity and non-conceptuality, whose results are emanation embodiment and Dharma embodiment. Thus, they say, differentiating conceptualization is the play of primordial knowing.

<!-- pair: TGD-003007 -->
Here, if it is claimed that mantra’s path transforms nonvirtue into virtue, there is no occasion for such transformation. Although the mandala wheel is primordial, adventitious stains obscure it, and the path removes or purifies those stains. Hevajra says:

<!-- pair: TGD-003012 -->
Sentient beings are buddhas,
Yet obscured by adventitious stains.
When those are removed, they are buddhas.

<!-- pair: TGD-003015 -->
Thus it is a path of purification. Moreover, in the vehicle of characteristics too, the Extensive Sutra’s silk scroll containing the three-thousandfold world and the Essence Sutra’s nine examples teach this and the like. [The supplied text then says:] it is not a path of abandonment; it is a path of transformation.[^N-B-080]

<!-- pair: TGD-003019 -->
If, unlike that, it were a path of transformation, it would conflict with “He is called Blessed One because he has conquered the Maras, thorough affliction and the rest.” The three poisons are not established as The Ground; if transformation changed them, nonvirtue would become virtue, contradicting the natural course of cause and result. Yet if it were abandonment [of The Ground], that would conflict with the permanent essence and would not be mantra. Therefore it is a path of purification.

<!-- pair: TGD-003026 -->
Vajra statement. Others say: “In mantra, practising nonvirtue with skillful means makes it virtue. Desire, hatred, stupidity, pride, and jealousy are not abandoned.”

<!-- pair: TGD-003029 -->
Here nonvirtue prohibited in discipline is nonvirtue and something to abandon in mantra too; it does not become virtue. No one in the world, including its gods, can refute what buddhas’ primordial knowing of the possible and impossible sees and declares an obstacle to the path. Moreover, Vajradhara’s scrambling of continuums, twisting of transmissions, and concealment of instructions, and the six limits, chiefly involve statements with enlightened intent and nonliteral words, as well as concealed-meaning and general explanations among the four modes.[^N-B-081]

<!-- pair: TGD-003038 -->
Thus the four samayas of Guhyasamaja’s four families, killing living beings and so forth, are explained in the Prediction of Enlightened Intent:

<!-- pair: TGD-003040 -->
What is not given is the ordinary mind of awakening;
Teaching the relative is speaking falsely.
The aggregates are the living beings;
The four Brahma abodes are the women.
When explained through enlightened intent,
The childish understand “as they wish.”
Though wishing to abide in the “lord-lord vehicle,”
The nature of phenomena is its primordial knowing.[^N-B-082]

<!-- pair: TGD-003048 -->
And:

<!-- pair: TGD-003049 -->
Those confused about words of enlightened intent,
Not realizing the meaning of what is said,
Say, “It is nothing other than this statement,”
And then take the speech literally.

<!-- pair: TGD-003053 -->
Thus acts connected with the three poisons are nonvirtuous even in mantra; without that connection, they are virtuous in mantra too.

<!-- pair: TGD-003055 -->
Addendum. Others say that people of inferior, middling, and superior faculty train respectively with the three scriptural collections as The Ground and individual liberation, bodhisattva training, and so forth as paths, attaining three results: peaceful happiness, nonabiding nirvana, and union’s great bliss.

<!-- pair: TGD-003058 -->
Here training in the vajra vehicle’s path stages requires all three, without removing or installing persons, teachings, results, and the rest as good or bad. They are the successive means, causes, and conditions for a single person to awaken, like a wise king’s establishing laws. The Two Examinations says, “First, confer restoration and purification,” and so forth.

<!-- pair: TGD-003065 -->
Therefore the three trainings are certainly required universally as stages of the path throughout the vows, including mantra vows.

<!-- pair: TGD-003066 -->
Addendum. Others say individual liberation, bodhisattva, and mantra are three distinct, different paths. Here the six perfections are the path of all three vehicles of sutra and mantra, because there is no way to awaken without relying on the two accumulations. The first three are the accumulation of merit, the last two the accumulation of primordial knowing, and diligence assists both. The glorious protector Nagarjuna’s Middle Way Precious Garland says:

<!-- pair: TGD-003072 -->
In brief, these teachings of the Buddha
Consist solely of the six perfections.

<!-- pair: TGD-003074 -->
This is known from that and other extensive teachings.

<!-- pair: TGD-003075 -->
[Not yet translated: U03075.]
